->The Title of the Creatures<-

All creatures shall call Him alone their Royal Majesty, Serene Highness, Well-born and High-born, Great and Mighty, Full of Grace, and the Lord of Glory, Most High and Honorable, Rabbi <note>original: "Rabbi und Meister"</note> and Master, and most honored LORD OF LORDS, and Redeemer, Savior, and Blessed Maker; they shall recognize Him as such, and not you!

Dear brothers! Where is your grace toward your fellow creatures? Let us see if it is equal to God’s grace! God calls Himself a father of widows and orphans; and He is so in deed. When they are in anguish, He comforts them; when they are sick, He is their physician; when they are poor, He is their wealth; when they are hungry, He fills them with good things; when they are in need and death, He is their life; and He rescues and helps all those who desire help from Him. Now we shall see how you compare yourselves with Him. I cannot compare you with Him, nor even with God’s Son! Indeed, you are like God’s adversary <note>A reference to the Devil or Satan</note>, and that in every respect—and almost worse than he himself. He is a destroyer; so are you! A murderer of souls; you are skinners <note>term: Schinder; literally those who flay animals, used here to describe those who ruthlessly exploit the poor</note> and murderers at the same time! Do you not skin your subjects? Do you not squeeze the poor? Do you not oppress the needy? I have seen that in many places they must be in your forced labor <note>term: Fron-Diensten; feudal labor obligations owed by a peasant to a lord</note> for 1, 2, 3, up to 4 days throughout the week! Besides this, they must give so much in taxes <note>term: Schatzung; direct land or property tax</note>, so much in excise <note>term: Accis; consumption tax on goods like food and drink</note>, so much in war-money, and on top of that, still more "gifts" <note>original: "Schenckaschen"; likely a corruption of the French 'gentillesse' or 'cadeau', referring to arbitrary tributes</note> in abundance. There is redemption-money <note>term: Ablöß-Geld; a fee paid to exempt oneself from a specific feudal duty</note> for grain, barley, wheat, cattle, sheep, oxen, wine, beer, and bread. Is that not a sin that cries out to heaven? Are the people not your slaves? Do you not strangle them in body, property, and blood? Not even so much can

# Rejection

...that the poor people have before them, so that they might pray the **Our Father** <note>The Lord's Prayer</note> with a single mind! And you are to blame for this, you **Regents** <note>Rulers or governing officials</note>, who make them into your own **slaves**! For you take from them what is theirs, because you live in **luxury**! Do you think you can stand before **God** in such a state? What is your **God** and **divine service**? Look at your hands, to see if they are not full of **blood**; and your **money-purses**, magnificent **clothes, carriages, horses, palaces, fields, vineyards**, and the like—see if they are not full of the **sweat-drops** of the **poor**! Certainly your **sin** has climbed into **heaven**, and the **blood** of the **poor** cries out against you! Oh and woe in all lands! For you are to blame for the **destruction of their souls and bodies!** Yet you say: you are **Christians.** If you are now **Christians**, then your **subjects** are also **Christians** and **brothers.** If they are now your **brothers**, why then do you make them into your **slaves**? Has **Christ** not called them to **freedom** just as well as you, if you are indeed **Christians**? I wish it for you from my **heart** that you were so: I would gladly become your **footstool** in **Christianity!**

### Further
## Concerning the Guilds and Craftsmen

Just as it stands with the **Estate of Regents**, so it also stands with our **Guilds** and **Craftsmen**.

Look, dear **brothers!** We allow ourselves to be made into **Masters** at great **expense**; and yet our **Mastery** is nothing but pure **foolishness**. Look! When we have applied so much cost to this **Master-Title**, then I ask you: Are we now thereby...

# The Title of Creatures
<note>original: "der Creaturen-Titul"</note>

...become any wiser or more sensible? Not at all! Have we been improved by it in any way? Also not at all! Are we not the same people we were before? If you have learned well, you will be able to do well and serve well. If you did not learn it before, you will certainly not learn it afterwards through the **Master-Title**. And why do we call ourselves Masters? For we are all together nothing but **bunglers**! <note>original: "Stümpler"; implying unskilled or clumsy workers compared to the Divine</note> For the true **Master-Builder** <note>original: "Werckmeister"</note> is God’s Wisdom, through which all things are created. Is it not so? Test it! I know that I do not lie! But if we are "Masters," as we call ourselves and allow ourselves to be titled, why then can we not make everything? If I were a Master, I would have to be able to create everything. And if I cannot make everything that I see and that is here on earth at hand, why then do I let myself be called Master? As it is, however, all our doing is **piecemeal**, <note>original: "Stückwerck"; a reference to 1 Corinthians 13:9, suggesting human knowledge is incomplete</note> and every man lacks the art and science of the other.

See, dear brothers! Does it not run contrary to Nature that we conduct ourselves thus? Is it not so; look! When you have come to this Title through great expense—whether you understand something or nothing at all—you then begin to persecute another who perhaps does not wish to accept this Master-Title, even if he understands more than all of you! Is it not so? Yes, certainly! You call him a **bread-thief** <note>original: "Brod-Dieb"; a derogatory term for an artisan working outside the guild system</note> alongside you! Is what you do right? You are in the wrong! Why do you begrudge him the bread that he has earned with bitter sweat through manual labor, just as you have? Was he not made by the hand of God just as you were? If he now wishes to nourish himself by the work of his hands, just as you do, why then do you forbid him? For God commanded him after the **Fall of Man** <note>original: "Sünden-Fall"</note> that he should work!

<summary>The author argues that human titles like "Master" are vain "piecemeal" compared to God's wisdom, and criticizes guilds for persecuting non-titled workers who are simply obeying God's command to labor.</summary>

Indeed, the LORD has forbidden it when He says: **You shall not let yourselves be called Master; for one is your Master, Christ, the Wisdom through whom all things were created**

### One should not let oneself be driven by the vain spirit of thoughts.

<margin>1.</margin> He who lets himself be driven by the spirit of thoughts <note>The "Gedancken-Geist" refers to the restless, discursive intellect that distracts from spiritual stillness.</note> in this life /
Is like the winds / which can remain nowhere.

### The spirit of the soul in seeking desire is like a musician.

<margin>2.</margin> My spirit / the musician / tunes all its powers:
And when its King <note>Referring to the arrival of the Divine presence within the soul.</note> comes / it sings most lovely.

### The inner tongue is like a lute, or some other instrument.

<margin>3.</margin> The tongue is his lute / his psaltery and trumpets:
As soon as he makes music / everything is full of wonder.

### One should love the little fountain of God's soul.

<margin>4.</margin> If you love the little fountain / from which the soul flowed;
Then you love rightly and well / as a companion of His kingdom.

### God should be loved above all things.

<margin>5.</margin> If you love Him who loves you / I mean from the bottom of the soul;
Then you do / what God does / and you will never fail Him.

### The lark praises God with great immersion in Him.

<margin>6.</margin> The little lark in the air is quite in love with singing:
O how it immerses itself / when it brings praise to God!

### Four Cherubic powers are in man from God and the elements.

<margin>7.</margin> The four Cherubim <note>original: "Cherubinische Kräfften." In mystical tradition, these represent the four living creatures of Ezekiel's vision, mirrored within the human microcosm.</note> there / and the four elements /
Are God’s life-force / which are also to be found in us.

### Their 24 wings are monthly days / in which the four Cherubim are four Sabbaths.

<margin>8.</margin> The wings upon them are four weeks of work-days:
The Sabbath-rest gives reward / makes an end to the toil of labor.

# Mirror of Rhymes.

### The 24 Elders are fruits of the firstborn of the four weekdays.

<margin>9.</margin> The firstborn of the eleventh is God’s power and essence:
Every month brings fruit, lovely and exquisite. <note>The "24 Elders" refers to the figures in Revelation 4:4, here interpreted as spiritual fruits or stages of time.</note>

### Concerning the unfathomable sea of the sensory understanding.

<margin>10.</margin> The sea is wide and deep; I mean, the understanding and the senses.
Whoever wishes to know it, let him test it: the fullness of understanding is within.

### The third part of the earthly sea of senses must be struck.

<margin>11.</margin> The third part of the sun—the natural understanding in the heart—
Must be struck with melancholy, anguish, and pain. <note>A reference to Revelation 8:12, where celestial bodies are darkened; the author applies this to the "death" of the egoistic intellect.</note>

### The monthly sea of reason must also be struck.

<margin>12.</margin> The third part of the moon—natural understanding and wit—
Must suffer punishment and pain, anguish, misery, torment, and heat.

### The third part of the stellar powers must be struck.

<margin>13.</margin> The third part of the stars—the powers of flesh and blood—
Must be put to death through the earnest work of repentance. <term>Stellar powers: In Boehme-influenced mysticism, the "stars" often represent the astral influences or biological drives governing human nature.</term>

### The natural essence of these three parts must be born through the birth of anguish.

<margin>14.</margin> The third part of the three must be entirely darkened,
As much as the light of the flesh lives and shines upon this earth.

### Days spent uselessly in false security are lost.

<margin>15.</margin> The day of false security, and the night of sin likewise;
The time thus spent must fade away like death.

### The triple woe upon the sinners.

<margin>16.</margin> Oh woe, woe, woe to you, and to me just the same!
If those others did not escape, how shall we evade it?

### Longing of the soul for the love of God.

<margin>17.</margin> Ah, that your precious love, my God! might accomplish it, <gloss>The nasal bar over the 'e' in 'vollbringe' indicates the missing 'n'</gloss>
And crush and penetrate my heart, brain, and flesh!

# Mirror of Rhymes. 295

## On the Purity of the Spirit.

<margin>18.</margin> When the spirit of the heart is pure, it shines just like the sun /
With its teaching of life, and bears witness to the pure fountain <note>The "fountain" or "wellspring" is a common mystical metaphor for God as the source of all life.</note>.

## On the Purity of the Intellect.

<margin>19.</margin> When the spirit of the brain seeks God with sense, reason, and will /
It resembles the full moon, making the teachings of life overflow.

## The Stars of the Bodily Powers should be offered to God.

<margin>20.</margin> And if one brings the life-force to God as an offering /
It is like the stars, from which many a virtue springs forth.

## Through the Suffering of Christ’s Cross comes Salvation.

<margin>21.</margin> The little bee, which stings hard, gives sweet honey:
Much more shall eternal life follow upon Christ’s Cross.

## God should be praised for His blows.

<margin>22.</margin> The little nettle, which burns me, yet offers itself to me as food <note>Nettles were commonly used in folk medicine and as a spring vegetable; the author uses this to show how divine discipline serves a purpose.</note>:
Much more God, who strikes me, for which I give thanks and praise.

## God is not angry with us forever, and does not cast us off entirely.

<margin>23.</margin> Look at the little thorn-bush, which often scratches and stings /
Yet, when Spring comes, it bids us pluck the roses.

## The soul is a fire within man, which is fanned and nourished by the spirit of the air.

<margin>24.</margin> The soul is the fire, longing, torment, and smoke:
The flame of love is precious; yet a breath fans it upward.

## Man has his life from God and the universal astral atmosphere.

<margin>25.</margin> The bellows draw the wind; from which the soul receives life:
I mean, to the child of Adam <note>A traditional term for humanity in its fallen or natural state.</note>, the soul is given by God.

## Sin has made man into a desolate field.

<margin>26.</margin> The wilderness is great in all of Adam’s children:
Ah, take root, and root out whatever would diminish the Good!

# Mirror of Rhymes.

## Christ has overcome sin / and desires to overcome it in us as well.

<margin>27.</margin>O God’s Lamb of Love! You have indeed overcome,
And also found for us an eternal redemption! <note>The author uses "found" (erfunden) in the archaic sense of obtaining or securing something through great effort.</note>

## His precious blood
## Gives strength / grants courage.

->us.<-

## On the misuse of noble time.

<margin>29.</margin>Do not misuse time! It is not given for that purpose.
Whoever wishes to find pearls must strive in seeking them.

## On noble fortune and time.

<margin>30.</margin>Whoever loves good fortune must always strive for the best,
And must highly esteem the noble time in this world.

## Punishment for those who despise the Cross
## of Christ.

<margin>31.</margin>Whoever despises the dear Cross must receive blows in its stead:
And whoever remains a despiser is not worthy of God’s gifts.

## The Cross-loving wanderer.

<margin>32.</margin>A man who wishes to travel must endure storm and rain:
Yet he suffers it and remains still <note>In this context, "still" implies a quiet, patient resignation to God's will.</note> on all his paths.

## The foolish hunter
## after transitory wealth.

<margin>33.</margin>Many a man runs day and night, hoping to become rich through diligence:
And when he has achieved it, he dies / and must return to the earth.

## The brave hero’s-run toward God.

<margin>34.</margin>Run always like a hero! No one will be crowned
Unless he has fought rightly: such a one is the richest on earth.

## The persistent struggle-run toward God.

<margin>35.</margin>Think of the beggar who sits by the roadside,
Who often persists in vain: yet he remains and labors. <note>original: "schwitzet" (sweats). This suggests the physical and mental exertion of persistent prayer or waiting.</note>

## Further persistence in the run.

<margin>36.</margin>Look at the peasant, how bitter he lets life be for himself!
He plows, sows, and harrows, and has nothing but hardships.

<meta>catchword: After</meta>

<summary>This page contains a series of rhymed devotional verses (numbers 27-36) emphasizing the necessity of spiritual discipline, the proper use of time, and the patient endurance of suffering as a "heroic run" toward God.</summary>
<keywords>redemption, blood of Christ, stewardship of time, theology of the cross, spiritual perseverance, vanity of wealth, spiritual metaphors</keywords>

--- Page 351 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Mirror of Rhymes," a collection of moralizing emblems or aphorisms. The previous page likely contained similar numbered verses (1-36) offering spiritual guidance through metaphors.</meta>

# Mirror of Rhymes.

### After great toil and labor, he must still give tithes.
<margin>37.</margin> And when he wishes to harvest, he must give interest-tithes <note>original: "Zinß-zeh'nden"; a double burden of both rent/interest and the church tithe</note>.
Much more, you child of God! pay your faithfulness with your life.

---

### The Gold-seeking Lover.
<margin>38.</margin> Whoever wishes to find pure gold must first study Nature:
Otherwise "Nothing" will be his reward, and he shall lose everything.

---

### The Turbulent Sea of the Heart.
<margin>39.</margin> A sea becomes turbulent through wild storms and winds:
A bitter heart just as bad, through quarreling, anger, strife, and sin.

---

### The Little Ship of Faith hovering in Danger.
<margin>40.</margin> The little ship upon the sea must endure much danger:
Now robbers attack it; now it is about to sink to the bottom.

---

### The Brave Warrior and Overcomer.
<margin>41.</margin> Fight bravely, like a man, against your flesh and sins!
You must constantly persevere; thus you shall overcome!

---

### Horses, Lions, Serpents: in what should one imitate them?
<margin>42.</margin> Be courageous like a horse; and stronger than the lions;
Wise and clever, more than a serpent; then God is your life.

---

### In what should one not?
<margin>43.</margin> Only flee the serpent’s poison, and also the pride of horses,
And the lion’s ferocity, all your lifelong on earth!

---

### Warning against Avarice and Arrogance.
<margin>44.</margin> Flee arrogance <term>Hoffart: an archaic term for excessive pride or haughtiness</term>, avoid sin and avarice like a serpent!
The pleasure of the rich man does not last long in the world.

---

### Warning against False Security and Pomp.
<margin>45.</margin> Flee false security and pomp, high-living, and banqueting;
Heed not gluttony: they lead to the Devil.

---

### Warning against more other Vices.
<margin>46.</margin> Flee lust, passion, and cunning; avoid heavy drinking, gorging, and gambling!
For no Christian does such things: whoever does them will feel vengeance.

<meta>catchword: For</meta>

<summary>This page contains a series of numbered moral aphorisms (37-46) using metaphors like the sea, ships, and animals to warn against vices such as pride, greed, and gluttony. It emphasizes the necessity of spiritual struggle and the fleeting nature of worldly wealth.</summary>

<keywords>morality, aphorisms, spiritual warfare, vices, virtues, metaphors, Christian life, pride, avarice, temperance</keywords>

--- Page 352 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Mirror of Rhymes," a collection of devotional poetic warnings and exhortations. The previous page likely contained similar numbered stanzas (1-46) focusing on spiritual discipline.</meta>

# Mirror of Rhymes.

### Against all Vanity, Foolish Pranks, and Jesting Speech.

<margin>47.</margin> Flee the world's vanity, foolish pranks, and jesting speech;
Shut your door against them: fight through in all distress! <note>The author emphasizes "fighting through" (durchkämpfen), a common Pietist term for the spiritual struggle toward salvation.</note>

### Against False Love, Praises, and Caresses.

<margin>48.</margin> Flee beauty and false love; flee praises and caresses;
Flee all human trifles; do not go picking roses with them! <note>The "picking of roses" is a metaphor for indulging in worldly or carnal pleasures.</note>

### Warning against the Misuse of all Created Things:

<margin>49.</margin> The misuse of the creature <note>original: "Creatur"; refers to any created thing or being in the physical world.</note> in all things on earth,
Flee; also avoid what is permitted, often; suffer hardship instead!

### Against the Favor of Lords, Riches, and the Honor of the World.

<margin>50.</margin> Flee the favor and grace of lords; flee offices, riches, and honors:
Flee what the Devil possesses; flee what can lead you astray!

### Against Courtly Life and the Spirit of Commerce.

<margin>51.</margin> Courtly life and the business of trade—from these you must flee far!
For this spirit has power; otherwise, it will pull you away forever.

### Against all Manner of Lust and Delicacies.

<margin>52.</margin> Flee all delicacies; choose rather to suffer want:
The spirit of lust must be combated in this manner.

### Exhortation to Observe the Noble Time.

<margin>53.</margin> Observe the noble time; buy up the hours of freedom! <note>A reference to the biblical concept of "redeeming the time" (Ephesians 5:16).</note>
The Judge is not far: well is it for him who has found Jesus!

### What One Should Buy:

<margin>54.</margin> Buy every moment; buy truth, peace, and love:
Buy these pieces for nothing: do not grieve God any longer!

### What More?

<margin>55.</margin> Buy wisdom, light, and justice; buy God's favor and faith:
Yes, buy a simple life, and the innocence of pious doves?

<meta>catchword: What</meta>

<summary>This page contains a series of rhymed devotional stanzas (47-55) advising the reader to flee worldly distractions such as courtly life, commerce, and physical pleasures in favor of spiritual virtues. It emphasizes the "buying" of spiritual qualities like wisdom and truth while time remains before the final judgment.</summary>
<keywords>asceticism, vanity, spiritual discipline, Pietism, redeeming time, simplicity, rejection of the world</keywords>

### What more?

<margin>56.</margin> Purchase Freedom / Fear of GOD / Patience in all distress:
Guard yourself against the Devil’s mockery; and pray constantly in faith.

### What more?

<margin>57.</margin> Purchase a contented heart / that seeks God without deceit,
With true earnestness / with sorrow: so that you may not be
->cursed.<-

### What else should one purchase?

<margin>58.</margin> Purchase the Power of GOD’s Peace / purchase Christ’s Blood and
->Wounds:<-
Through this red Juice <note>A poetic reference to the blood of the atonement</note> all things are overcome.

### Summary: Everything is to be purchased from Christ through true Change of Mind.

<margin>59.</margin> Purchase all of this for yourself / Through Him / who earned it for you:
You need it as much / as I do: Die / just as He has died! <note>Refers to the mystical "death of the self" or dying to sin.</note>

### Burial and Decay in Christ’s Death.

<margin>60.</margin> I must } be buried / decay in Christ’s death:
You must }
If otherwise { my } soul is to recover in Christ’s Light.
{ your }

# A Spiritual Song

->2.<-
->*(The heart, dying to the world and living in God, overflows.)*<-

62. My heart overflows <note>original: "wallet"; suggests a bubbling or surging emotion</note> toward You always, even when it is in mortal danger. I am assured of Your faithfulness, which makes my joy new every day. Hallelujah!

->3.<-
->*(The heart assures itself of Jesus, to whom it is bound.)*<-

63. Therefore I will be joyful, as long as I know that You are mine, bound to me at every time and hour, and that You dwell in the depths of my heart. Hallelujah!

->4.<-
->*(On account of His obligation and the pledging of all His honor through the Word: He does not desire the death of the sinner, but that he should convert and live.)*<-

64. You have indeed deeply obligated Yourself, and pledged all Your honor as security! <note>A reference to Ezekiel 33:11, a common Lutheran comfort regarding God's promise of mercy.</note> How then can it be otherwise, than that I am Yours, and You are mine? Hallelujah!

->5.<-
->*(Therefore the heart praises and glorifies the majestic name of God.)*<-

65. Praised and greatly glorified be Your faithful Name for this, with praise, might, and power at every hour! May the depths of my heart be full of You! Hallelujah!

# A Spiritual Song.

->6.<-
->(And he desires to glorify Him with his own death through the death of Christ, his Lord.)<-

66. Now I wish to die to myself daily, and so glorify Your honor. Ah yes, now I must be truly earnest, for the five little hours <note>likely referring to the final hours of life or a specific liturgical watch</note> are no longer far away! Hallelujah! ->7.<-
->(May the second fivefold death-wound in my Jesus give me strength!)<-

67. Ah, nail all my desires within me, in my left foot, to the cross—the desires of my own self-righteousness! <term>Self-righteousness: the spiritual pride of believing one is saved by their own works rather than grace.</term> Ah, how heartily I regret it! Hall. But You are nonetheless my joy. Hallelujah!

->8.<-
->(May the third fivefold death-wound in my Jesus give me strength!)<-

68. Ah, strengthen my right foot through true repentance and a change of heart! So that I may be holy in You, and Your righteousness may become mine. Hall. May Your Spirit also make my heart pure! Hallelujah!

->9.<-
->(May the fourth fivefold death-wound in my Jesus give me strength!)<-

69. Ah, crucify the fire of my zeal—the evil within my left hand! Yes, yes, I give you no more power; <insert>Your / My</insert> time is up; soon, good night! Hallel. For the roof of my life <note>an idiom for the end of one's earthly days</note> hastens toward its end. Hallelujah!

# A Spiritual Song.

### 10.

->(**May the fifth mortal wound in my**<-
->**JESUS give me strength!**)<-

70. Oh, strengthen my right hand upon the Cross / and cover my shame / and grant me Your purity / that beautiful garment of Paradise! <note>original: "Paradises-Kleid"; a reference to the state of innocence restored through Christ's sacrifice.</note> Hallelujah.

Thus the time of my life is fulfilled. Hallelujah!
In pure delight and gratitude. Hallelujah!
Thus I take my leave of the world. Hallelujah!
And behold **GOD’S** glory. Hallelujah!
And His eternal time of crowning. Hallelujah!
Also His beautiful loveliness. Hallelujah!
And His great mercy. Hallelujah!
After the time of crucifixion has been endured. Hallelujah.
May **GOD** give us all such joy! Hallelujah!
And to my brothers at all times! Hallelujah!
->To praise Him in eternity!<-
->Hallelujah!<-
